#2696. Iran war dents Middle East sports model as events are canceled or postponed. F1 bucks the trend

Two season-ending Formula 1 races in the Middle East are going ahead despite the war in Iran and retaliatory attacks across the region that have canceled or postponed other large sports events. This includes two Formula 1 races earlier this year in Saudi Arabia and Bahrain.

The conflict has upended the region's image as a safe haven where governments and sponsors have kicked in billions to lure mega-sports events. This comes as Europe and North America appear wary of such lavish spending.

"The situation could change but the geography will not change," Sebastian Sons, who follows sports in the Middle East with the German research institute CARPO, told The Associated Press. "The Middle East and the Gulf region are always a volatile and fragile region."

Going ahead is an endorsement for doing business in the region, but has also prompted concerns about safety from some teams and staff. Mohammed Ben Sulayem, the president of auto racing's world governing body FIA, has said "we are fully prepared" to pull off the two races: Qatar on Nov. 29 and Abu Dhabi on Dec. 6.

Ben Sulayem is from the United Arab Emirates.

A MotoGP race was held in Qatar on Nov. 8 on the track where F1 races. MotoGP is operated by Liberty Media, which also runs F1.

"Holding the races in Qatar and Abu Dhabi signals that those two are stable and able to host large-scale sporting events. It is important they are seen to be open for business," Kristian Ulrichsen, a political scientist at Rice University's Baker Institute, told The AP.

"Predictability in long-term decision-making and policy planning," Ulrichsen added, "made the region so attractive as a host of mega-events at a time when many cities and countries around the world are rethinking the cost of doing so."

Qatar's reputation is on the line. It held the 2022 World Cup and is a co-favorite — with India — to land the 2036 Olympics. It's also host next year to the basketball World Cup (Aug. 27-Sept. 12) and has the 2030 Asian Games.

It took a hit last week when the United States pulled its team from the boys' under-17 World Cup being played in Qatar.

Saudi Arabia and its Persian Gulf neighbors have spent big to hold major sports events but were cutting back on mega-sports funding before the war began nine months ago.

"For Saudi, the war has accelerated the need to find economies, to maybe reassess what was feasible and what wasn't," Ulrichsen said. He said landing the "big prize," two years ago — the 2034 World Cup — probably honed Saudi's focus.

"Qatar and the UAE will probably have to at some point do the same," he added.

Major events called off, postponed or relocated include the two Formula 1 races earlier in the season, and the WTA season-ending tennis finals and soccer's monthlong Asian Cup — both originally scheduled for Saudi Arabia. The 2027 Dakar Rally was also moved from Saudi Arabia to Portugal and Morocco.

The region has been using sports to promote "soft power" to enhance its brand. But Saudi Arabia now faces hard power with Yemen's Iran-backed Houthi rebels having struck Saudi Arabia's main international airport in Riyadh. Riyadh's business district has also recently been targeted.

The war has also affected the large Gulf air carriers and changed travel patterns in the region.

"One of the things Iran did during the war was to deliberately attack the connective tissue between Gulf states and the world," Sascha Bruchmann, an analyst at the International Institute for Strategic Studies, told The AP. "This includes ports, airports, hotels and data centers — signaling that Iran can attack important issues beyond hydrocarbons and challenge the Gulf states' ambitions to diversify."

Sons, the researcher at the German think tank, estimated that Saudi Arabia invested more than $6 billion in international sports between 2021 and 2023. Sons said the country hosted more than 100 international sports events between 2019 and 2024.

A visible failure was the bankruptcy of LIV Golf, which reports say was funded by approximately $5 billion from Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund. Saudi Arabia also canceled the 2029 Asian Winter Games, planned for the Trojena ski village in the south of the desert kingdom.

The event has been moved to Almaty, Kazakhstan, which lost to Beijing in a close vote for the 2022 Winter Olympics.

Ulrichsen said Saudi Arabia was backing off what seemed like a "scattergun approach," backing LIV Golf, planning an unlikely Winter Asian Games, bankrolling soccer star Cristiano Ronaldo, and buying the Premier League team Newcastle. All are varied approaches to "soft power."

Ronaldo is reported to earn more than $200 million annually with Saudi club Al-Nassr.

Qatar's sovereign wealth fund controls French club Paris Saint-Germain, and Manchester City is controlled by an Abu Dhabi investment group. Ulrichsen argued sports teams are a more effective investment than World Cups or Olympics "because people care more about the teams they support than an event that happens for three or four weeks."

"When Ronaldo would post on Instagram about the amazing lifestyle he was enjoying in Riyadh, that would reach upward of a half a billion people," Ulrichsen added. "You simply can't buy that kind of reach with a conventional PR campaign."

#2694. The Kevin O'Connell-Jets connection resurfaces, not surprisingly

As the Jets move toward their inevitable next coaching search, folks who cover the Jets have begun to compile the list of possibilities for the successor to Aaron Glenn.

Rich Cimini, who covers the Jets for ESPN, planted the flag on Vikings coach Kevin O’Connell being “the name I would watch for.”

It’s not the first time the connection has been made. Two years ago, when O’Connell was entering the final season of his first contract in Minnesota and, on the day the Vikings were preparing to play the Lions for the No. 1 seed in the NFC playoff field, Jay Glazer of Fox Sports reported that O’Connell could be traded to a new team. As Darren Mougey emerged as one of the top candidates to become the Jets’ new G.M., the obvious dots were being connected to O’Connell, given that Mougey and O’Connell were roommates at San Diego State.

Cimini’s recent take is premised on the Vikings falling apart down the stretch and/or the team deciding to elevate defensive coordinator Brian Flores to head coach before he gets hired by another team.

Obviously, if the Vikings were independently considering moving on from O’Connell, it would make sense to get compensation by trading him to a new team. But there’s no reason to think the Vikings are considering moving on from O’Connell.

While the “quarterback whisperer” vibe has changed significantly since the start of the 2025 season, the blame for the revolving door of players who ultimately weren’t good enough falls on plenty of people. After the 2024 season, when the Vikings had to pick a starter from Sam Darnold, Daniel Jones, and J.J. McCarthy, it appears that someone other than O’Connell made the call to let Darnold and Jones leave and to entrust the job to McCarthy, who had played only one preseason game and nothing more in his rookie season.

The fact that the Vikings fired G.M. Kwesi Adofo-Mensah after the 2025 season suggests that the organization did a deep dive into the factors that scrapped an all-in financial commitment that failed to result in the franchise’s first back-to-back playoff appearances since 2008 and 2009, and that they put the blame for the unfulfilled expectations on him.

O’Connell’s preferences would be a factor, too. Much of that depends on his relationship with new G.M. Nolan Teasley.

As to Flores, the chances of another team hiring him to be a head coach seem to be small, given his pending lawsuit against the league and four teams (the Dolphins, Giants, Broncos, and Texans). While it would be wrong (legally and morally) for the league at large to hold the litigation against Flores, he has already been snubbed multiple times since the case was filed. And that Korn Ferry document that recently surfaced specifically calls the ongoing legal fight a potential “distraction.”

The other question is whether Flores would jump to another team as a defensive coordinator, if the aversion to hiring him as a head coach would not keep another team from giving him that role.

Then there’s the Mougey angle. Cimini said that Mougey has spoken highly of O’Connell in internal meetings with the Jets. (Awkward, if Glenn was in the room when those comments were made.) As it becomes more clear that Glenn isn’t long for Gang Green, Mougey has a clear incentive to devise a plan based on him sticking around and not being supplanted and/or significantly undermined (see the Giants) by a new coach who would want to hire his own G.M. O’Connell could be Mougey’s Hail Mary.

Beyond that, teams don’t often trade draft capital for head coaches (even though they should). Whether it’s rooted in honor among oligarchs or it’s a manifestation of the effort to remove power from the coaches (which has primarily occurred through efforts to hold down their pay), it doesn’t happen nearly as frequently as it could or should.

For now, it’s all speculation. And, as history shows us, the Jets are going to Jet. They’ve done nothing in recent years to create the impression that they’re capable of identifying and hiring the best candidate to coach the team.

Until they do, there’s no reason to assume they’ll suddenly get it right.

#2692. Jesse Minter: "Unacceptable" that we haven't been able to run the ball consistently

The Ravens have lost five straight games since a 4-1 start to the season and a look at the team’s results running the ball during the two halves of their season presents a big reason why their fortunes have changed.

Baltimore ran for an average of 164.4 yards per game in their first five contests and they’ve averaged 86.8 yards per game on the ground in the last five. The latter number would be even less impressive without a 65-yard touchdown by Justice Hill late in a blowout loss to the Jaguars and Derrick Henry’s current average of 3.8 yards per carry is almost two yards less than he managed during his first two seasons in Baltimore.

Things hit a new low on Monday night when the Ravens had 16 rushing yards in their loss to the previously winless Chargers. On Tuesday, head coach Jesse Minter didn’t point to one specific reason why the team has lost its way but he made it clear that it needs to change.

“There’s to me just a combination of a lot of factors that we need to figure out how to be able to run the football,” Minter said, via the team’s website. “There’s been spurts of it, but the last few games, it’s obviously not where it needs to be. It starts with me, starts with the coaches, goes down to the players. [It’s] unacceptable, honestly, that we’re not able to. [In] the first half, to have no yards rushing, it’s embarrassing. And so, we’ll keep trying to find a better way to do it.”

One obvious reason for the change is that Lamar Jackson was the quarterback for four of the first five games, which Minter referenced and rejected on Tuesday before saying he still believes the team has players who can get the job done. He followed that up by noting “talk is cheap” and the Ravens will have to find their way if they’re going to have any chance of reversing their playoff fortunes in the final weeks of the season.

#2690. Prep basketball roundup: Sherman Oaks Notre Dame holds off Palisades in high-scoring opener

As opening games go in high school basketball, Tuesday night's matchup between preseason No. 2-ranked Sherman Oaks Notre Dame and No. 12 Palisades produced a game that proved both teams are headed for lots of victories this season.

In the end, Notre Dame, despite playing much of the second half without standout guard NaVorro Bowman (shoulder injury) held on for a 94-87 victory. The teams combined to have nine players score in double figures and make 26 threes.

Junior guard Zion Lanier, who has been earning rave reviews at Notre Dame practices from coach Matt Sargeant, scored a career-high 26 points. "I had a meeting before the season with coach," he said. "I had to adjust to a new role and get out of my shell."

Bowman had 18 points before leaving the game in the third quarter. His arm was in a sling. Ilan Nikolov made four threes in the third quarter and finished with 18 points. Sophomore Nico Mecilli came off the bench to score a career-high 16 points and Josiah Nance had 14 points. Notre Dame made 15 threes.

Palisades, which was down 18 points at halftime, made a determined second-half comeback and left little doubt that it will be an overwhelming favorite to defend its City Section Open Division title. Sophomore guard Phil Reed scored 15 of his 26 points in third quarter. EJ Popoola had 20 points, OJ Popoola 18 and Jack Levey 17.

The Dolphins have aspirations of challenging top Southern Section teams, and Tuesday's performance sent a clear message they can do it. They play St. Francis on Saturday.

"We dug ourselves a hole," Palisades coach Jeff Bryant said. "We made proper adjustments and fought back in the second half and came up short. That was a shootout. That's why we schedule these games to see where we're at."

St. John Bosco 83, Newport Harbor 27: David Conerly led the way with 16 points and Chace Patterson had 14 points.

Milken 79, St. Monica 63: Grayson Coleman started his senior season with a 35-point performance.

Harvard-Westlake 93, St. Genevieve 35: Alex Floyd scored 20 points, Nairobi Ebi 17 and Pierce Thompson 15 for the Wolverines, who also received seven assists from Cole Holden.

Anaheim Canyon 78, South Torrance 50: Tolu Akomolafe made seven threes and finished with 27 points.

Damien 81, Torrance 39: Zaire Rasshan had 22 points for Damien (2-0).

Etiwanda 64, Rancho Verde 45: Devin Mitchell had 34 points for the Eagles.

Village Christian 107, El Camino Real 39: Sophomore Will Conroy Jr. had 30 points and 11 rebounds and Deion Lewis added 24 points for the Crusaders.

Sherman Oaks Notre Dame 73, Palisades 29: Sophomores Alexa Castillo (15 points) and Elsie Hodge (12 points) led the Knights. Freshman Cali Bowman scored 12 points in her high school debut. She's the sister of boys' star NaVorro Bowman.

Oaks Christian 74, Oxnard 47: Presley Kushner scored 28 points and Bonita Liu added 23 points.

#2689. Knicks pull off Toronto sweep thanks to Jalen Brunson’s late 4-point play

TORONTO — The Knicks channeled their inner Drake by going "Back to Back" in Toronto.

With Canada's famous rapper sitting courtside inside Scotiabank Arena, Jalen Brunson again snatched victory from the Raptors with a momentous late sequence.

The point guard pulled up for a 3-pointer with 1:25 remaining in a close game, which he buried while being flagrantly fouled because defender Ja'Kobe Walter stepped into Brunson's landing space.

The result was a 4-point play for Brunson and possession retained by the Knicks, who were fouled and quickly made two more free throws, courtesy of Landry Shamet, to basically put away their 111-102 victory.

Jalen Brunson shoots a 3-pointer over Ja'Kobe Walter en route to a 4-point play late in the fourth quarter of the Knicks' 111-102 win over the Raptors on Nov. 17, 2026, in Toronto. Dan Hamilton-Imagn Images

That six-point swing meant the Knicks left Canada with two wins in three days.

"That created the separation that we needed to be able to put them away," coach Mike Brown said. "So just a big shot. It was one of those things where the play broke down, and we kept looking for an opportunity while spacing the floor. And Jalen found one and let it fly."

Brunson crumbled to the floor because of a twisted ankle, but bounced up, walked to the bench while the refs reviewed the foul, and recovered to hit the foul shot.

"Adrenaline was pumping," he said, "and went back out there."

It followed Brunson's big trey to beat the Raptors on Sunday, reinforcing the Knicks' dominance over their division foe.

The Knicks (8-6) have won 15 straight against the Raptors (5-9), a streak dating to 2023. But Toronto entered Tuesday confident about its chances after stretching the Knicks into overtime Sunday.

Karl-Anthony Towns, who had 16 points and 16 rebounds, slams home a dunk during the Knicks' road win over the Raptors. Dan Hamilton-Imagn Images

"After rewatching and rewatching and rewatching film, we played against the NBA champions and, in my 3 ¹/₂ years here as coach, that's when we came the closest to beating them," Raptors coach Darko Rajaković said. "We're getting better. Just one free throw and the game is completely different."

He finished with 31 points in 34 minutes on 10-for-22 shooting. Karl-Anthony Towns recorded his 14th straight double-double to start the season — a franchise record — dropping 16 points with 16 rebounds.

OG Anunoby, who scored 16 points, slams home a dunk during the Knicks' road win over the Raptors. Getty Images

The Knicks closed the game with a 9-0 run, which followed their 19-0 run to end the first half.

After struggling in the clutch to start the season, the Knicks rediscovered their clutch gene in Toronto.

"A lot of times when we get in games like that, especially last year, the cardiac Knicks find a way," Towns said.

Bridges put together a third consecutive strong performance with 17 points in 30 minutes. Josh Hart scored his first points in three games, dropping five in 31 minutes. Landry Shamet continued his hot November with 12 points and was a team-best plus-26.

After a four-day stop in Canada, Brown left satisfied with a team win — solidified by an important Brunson moment.

"I thought our execution, especially down the stretch, the last few minutes of the game, was at a pretty high level," the coach said, "on both ends of the floor, but particularly offensively. Our pacing, our spacing, our screening — we took care of the basketball down the stretch.

Jalen Brunson, who scored a game-high 31 points, shoots over Alijah Martin during the Knicks' road win over the Raptors. Dan Hamilton-Imagn Images

"We got a lot of different guys that produced in a lot of different ways tonight. Obviously, KAT continues to be a double-double machine. OG [Anunoby] was our defensive player of the game. … Mikal was good for us, especially down the stretch. And Landry Shamet was huge."

#2686. Rich Eisen Reflects on His Return to ESPN and Planting More Flags For The Future

Last year, Rich Eisen received a phone call he never imagined could happen. For the first time since 2003, the famed ESPN SportsCenter anchor returned to the place where his sports media career began its national rise. Twenty-two years after leaving to become the first personality on the NFL Network, Eisen returned with his own sports radio brand as The Rich Eisen Show joined ESPN Radio, with additional distribution on ESPN+ and Disney+.

Now, more than a year later, Eisen finds himself enjoying the opportunities his return to ESPN has provided. He has gained additional distribution, new teammates and opportunities to create original programming. Ultimately, Eisen says there is simply nothing like being back home with the worldwide leader in sports.

"I enjoy delivering the program into the ESPN, Disney+ sphere of influence and distribution. There is nothing like the ESPN platform. I experienced it from 1996-2003 as a host. But never thought my career would morph into host/small business owner," said Eisen. "After taking my show over in 2019 and building it, getting the opportunity to have that be the vehicle that returns me to ESPN, only to then have ESPN acquire my broadcasting rights from NFL Network. That's stuff you can't make up."

Eisen now serves in a hybrid role that combines elements of his previous ESPN experience with his current business ventures. He remains an on-air talent, hosting several programs on NFL Network throughout the calendar year, while also serving as the CEO and founder of Rich Eisen Productions.

The Rich Eisen Show remains Eisen's property. He still owns and produces the program, maintaining complete autonomy over its content. That was one of several promises ESPN made in its efforts to bring Eisen back home to the worldwide leader in sports.

"Everything I was promised I received in the first year, including support from ESPN digital and social platforms. Good Karma Sports selling my show and opening partnerships. Basically, fire hosing the show. They've done everything they say they would," noted Eisen. "It's been an awesome feeling."

Over the past year, Eisen has also had several opportunities to expand his presence under the ESPN umbrella. In April, he returned to ESPN's headquarters in Bristol, Connecticut, to host SportsCenter for the first time in more than two decades. He also worked with ESPN's archives on a podcast series that tapped into the nostalgia surrounding former SportsCenter anchors.

Most recently, ESPN announced that Eisen's radio program would expand its reach on Disney+. For the first time, The Rich Eisen Show would become available to international audiences through Disney+, with distribution in select countries across Europe, the Middle East and Africa (EMEA).

"I'm a professional flag planter. I was the first guy to be on the set of the NFL Network with a microphone. Everything NFL Network did growing up, any time we did something for the first time, I was thankfully handed the reins to lead it on air," said Eisen. "I planted flags for my show with DirecTV. Then I planted flags all over the place when I owned it. So, when ESPN wants to put my show and expand it on Disney+, great. I don't think about it as I have a lot of responsibilities. It's just what I'm used to doing."

For Eisen, expanding into international markets made complete sense. During his travels covering and calling NFL games overseas for NFL Network, he saw firsthand the interest and passion international audiences have for American football.

"I've seen the audience in Frankfurt, Munich, and London with my own two eyes," explained Eisen. "When we do a deal with my show with Disney and ESPN, they have worldwide rights. It makes sense for us to inquire about their expanding strategy, to see if we could get worldwide rights. To the credit of everyone at Disney+ and ESPN, they're up for it. Let's see how it works."

Calling NFL games overseas proved to be a rewarding experience for Eisen. He enjoyed more than the opportunity to broadcast the games themselves. He also embraced the atmosphere surrounding the events, from hosting The Rich Eisen Show on location to occasionally taking a Saturday stroll through a local beer garden.

However, 2026 brought a change. With ESPN overseeing NFL Network as part of its acquisition agreement with the NFL, Eisen's time calling the league's international series came to an end. He acknowledges that the assignment differed significantly from his typical responsibilities, but he admits he misses the experience.

"For the last year, ESPN and Disney provided our show a studio overseas. It was fun, we brought some of the staff. But, I got three kids at home and my role with NFL Network too," said Eisen. "But yes, I do miss it. One time in a week off between Frankfurt games, I went to Berlin and did a bike tour of the city because my wife insisted on getting out of the hotel when I was on my own. But, what a great experience overall."

Above all, Eisen says he loves radio. He appreciates the opportunity to connect with people in real time and share conversations about their common passion for sports. He also recognizes how fortunate he is that the hard work of his entire team continues to earn support from the audience.

That connection gives Eisen a different sense of satisfaction from growing his radio program than he gets from anything he accomplishes on television.

"I'm growing a business. I have people that depend on me, and I depend on them. Having an outlet, I love having that and tracking our success on our platform," said Eisen. "On the television side of things, we're always looking for new ways to break down the fourth wall and connect with the fan and meet them where they are. Radio is the literal sense of meeting the fans where they are because they can connect with you from anywhere in real time."

Another way Eisen has strengthened that connection is through a podcast that tugs at the heartstrings of longtime SportsCenter viewers. For years, he wanted to develop a series that explored the stories behind the scenes and revisited memorable moments with the voices who once shared the desk on ESPN's flagship highlights program.

After noticing the growing popularity of nostalgia-driven podcasts, Eisen pitched the concept to ESPN President Jimmy Pitaro during discussions about his return to the network.

"With my show coming back into the orbit of ESPN, I thought this was the perfect opportunity to execute what I always been thinking about," said Eisen. "Jimmy [Pitaro] loved the idea, and we were off to the races."

With access to ESPN's SportsCenter archives, Eisen and his coordinating producer, Michael Hoskyns, went to work. Eisen reached out to former SportsCenter colleagues to discuss memorable moments from the past, while Hoskyns navigated the archives, identified footage and helped conceptualize the final product.

In June, This Was SportsCenter launched with six episodes featuring notable ESPN anchors from the past. The response was overwhelming, and Eisen is already planning a second season.

"I have a few things in the hopper for next year, including a book. I've already reached out to a bunch of my old school colleagues that I saved for a potential second season. Either because they were unable to do season one, or wanted to see the popularity of season one," explained Eisen. "Doing it in person is so much better than not. There's nothing like a face-to-face chat."

Eisen credits much of the series' appeal to how dramatically SportsCenter has changed over the years. Even today, fans approach him with memories of staying up late at night or beginning their mornings with Eisen and his longtime partner, Stuart Scott.

"SportsCenter, the way it used to be is different than the way it is today. That also applies to so much of sports television," said Eisen. "I hear stories from fans who remember me and Stuart Scott all the time. So, doing This Was SportsCenter, or occasionally popping on SportsCenter today, it's a lane that I'm pleased to have. With my radio show and all the distribution we have now, I can ask someone if they remember when I had hair and life was different for me and you. I'm thrilled ESPN allows me to occupy that lane."

This February, ESPN will broadcast its first Super Bowl in network history. The event will likely represent the largest and most consequential broadcast in the network's history. Eisen hinted that his show will have a presence throughout Super Bowl week and on game day, particularly with his home studio based in Los Angeles.

"ESPN is the greatest platform there is in sports and entertainment. I can only imagine it will be a Super Bowlpalooza," noted Eisen. "I look forward to being a part of it. My show will be connected and we're creating our own environment in my studio here in Los Angeles like we did for the last Super Bowl here. We have some other plans about the show that I imagine will be announced really soon. But I'm excited about it, and I'll be a part of the Super Bowlpalooza. Whether it's my show or NFL Network, or presumably both. I'm really excited about it."

For Eisen, returning to ESPN wasn't about revisiting the past or trying to recapture what he once had. It was about finding a new way to build on everything that came before.

From launching NFL Network to growing his own radio show, creating original programming and expanding into international markets, Eisen has spent his career embracing opportunities that once seemed unimaginable.

Now, with a Super Bowl on the horizon, the NFL Combine and Draft approaching, and new projects taking shape, his second act with ESPN is still being written. And if there's one thing Eisen has learned throughout his career, it's that there are always more flags to plant.

#2683. 49ers-Cowboys cranks out 27.13 million viewers

Fox’s Sunday tripleheader culminated in the national telecast of the 8-1 49ers at the record-doesn’t-matter Cowboys.

On average, 27.13 million watched the 46-40 barnburner, which ultimately underscored the fact that: (1) the 49ers are elite; and (2) the old “Doomsday Defense” nickname has a much different meaning now.

The performance of the game reconfirms the fact that 4:25 p.m. ET has become the most valuable weekly window. It currently rotates between CBS and FOX, with multiple simultaneous games per year.

It’s hard not to imagine the NFL considering breaking that window away from the standard Sunday afternoon packages and selling it to one network. Given the size of the audiences, it would be the most lucrative package the NFL could offer.

This week, the NFL moved Steelers-Eagles from the late-afternoon window on CBS and shifted Jaguars-Giants into the spot. Raiders-Broncos also starts at 4:25 p.m. ET.

#2682. Spurs' winning streak is over after loss to Pistons

Victor Wembanyama and company fell to the Detroit Pistons 105-93 on Tuesday, Nov. 17 at Frost Bank Center, ending their 12-game win streak.

"Have fun. Play basketball. It's another game," Cunningham told NBC Sports after the game about the Pistons' mentality. "Obviously, they're a good team, but nobody in that locker room has fear in their heart for anybody. While heading downcourt, he slipped and fell, suffering an apparent groin injury.

Per the Spurs, they were on their 25th double-digit win streak since the 1976-77 season, their first in the NBA after moving from the ABA. This is the most streaks of more than 10 wins in the league, leading the Los Angeles Lakers (21), Boston Celtics (16), Cleveland Cavaliers (15) and Oklahoma City Thunder (14).

The NBA record for longest win streak is 33 games, which was set by the Lakers in the 1971-72 season.

Last week, Cunningham had a historic night in a win against the Washington Wizards. He scored a career-high 52 points and added 11 assists, five rebounds, a steal and a block. He did not commit a turnover. He was the first player in NBA history to notch at least 50 points and 10 assists in a game without turning the ball over.

#2680. AFC Playoff Picture: How standings, playoff odds look after Bengals blowout, Drake Maye injury

AFC Playoff Picture: How standings, playoff odds look after Bengals blowout, Drake Maye injury appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

As the NFL season passes the halfway point, the AFC playoff teams are starting to take hold of their positions. There are seven spots in the AFC playoffs, and it would not be a shocker if the teams that currently own those positions would finish the season as postseason teams.

The Buffalo Bills have the best record in the AFC, and they are rolling with an 8-1 record. The Bills have been motivated all season under first-year head coach Joe Brady. The Bills were angry a year ago when they lost their grip on the division and the New England Patriots took advantage of a fourth-place schedule and stole it from them. They appear to be quite secure in their position as they have a 3.5-game lead over the Pats (5-5), who have lost two in a row and saw QB Drake Maye go down with an ankle injury.

The Bills are plus-110 to emerge with the No. 1 seed in the AFC playoffs, according to DraftKings. The Bills are the only team likely to make the playoffs from the AFC East. The Patriots still have a chance, but they will be sending Tommy DeVito out to lead the attack, and he struggled badly after taking over for Maye in the Week 10 loss to the Detroit Lions.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

The Cincinnati Bengals (7-2) are on a major roll this season, and much of that is due to the health and dominance of QB Joe Burrow. The Bengals have won five games in a row, and Burrow has thrown for 2,795 yards with 27 TD passes and 6 interceptions. Based on his performance in the winning streak, Burrow looks unstoppable at this point. The Bengals are plus-600 to finish with the No. 1 seed in the AFC.

As dominant and exciting as the Bills and Bengals have been through the first half of the season, the Jacksonville Jaguars (7-2) have been nearly as good. The Jaguars have a 4.0-game lead over the Houston Texans (3-6) and they appear to be rolling to the AFC South title. Trevor Lawrence has reached elite status in the NFL, and he is having a brilliant year for head coach Liam Coen. Lawrence has completed 171 of 259 passes for 2,284 yards with a razor-sharp 21-5 TD-interception ratio.

Mandatory Credit: Steve Roberts-Imagn Images

The Jaguars are listed at plus-500 to end up with the No. 1 seed in the AFC playoffs.

The Bills, Bengals and Jaguars appear to have the best route to the playoffs and the top seed in the AFC. However, the Kansas City Chiefs and the Denver Broncos can't be counted out in the AFC West. Both teams are well on their way to making the playoffs and it's just a question of which of these 7-2 teams is going to emerge with the AFC West crown.

The Chiefs are demonstrating that last year's 6-11 finish was basically just a fluke The Chiefs have returned to excellent form and there is always confidence when Patrick Mahomes has the ball in his hands in a close game. The Chiefs are listed as minus-12500 to make the playoffs and plus-550 to earn the No. 1 seed in the AFC.

The Broncos appear to have a better shot at winning the AFC West crown over the Chiefs because their closing schedule is much easier. They play the Raiders twice, the Dolphins, the Jets and the Chargers, while the Chiefs have challenging games against the Bills, Rams and Bengals in consecutive weeks and another tough game against the 49ers. The Broncos are minus-8000 to make the AFC playoffs and plus-380 to take the No. 1 seed.

The Pittsburgh Steelers (6-3) and the surprising Cleveland Browns (6-4) are both in positions to take the final two seeds in the AFC playoffs. The Steelers suffered a 38-18 loss to the Bengals in Week 10, but head coach Mike McCarthy and QB Aaron Rodgers have no reason to panic. The Steelers are listed at minus-290 to make the playoffs. Their closing schedule features tough games against Denver and Jacksonville and winnable games against Philadelphia, Baltimore (2), Carolina and Tennessee.

The Browns currently have the No. 7 and final seed in the AFC playoffs. They have a 1.0-game lead over the Patriots, and while history says that it will be difficult for Cleveland to hold on to that playoff spot, first-year head coach Todd Monken has this team believing in itself even though this team is devoid of stars. The Browns are minus-130 to make the playoffs.

The Browns have been depending on Deshaun Watson at quarterback, and he has thrown for 2,118 yards and 7 TDs. He has also demonstrated that he can run away from pass rushers. He has 266 rushing yards and 2 TDs. The Browns are getting a lift from tight end Harold Fanin Jr. He has caught 44 passes for 544 yards and 4 TDs.

The struggling Patriots are the closest team that is out of the AFC playoff structure to gaining a position, but the absence of Maye means it is a major long shot Mike Vrabel's team to make the playoffs.

If the Browns are going to fall apart, it would seem that the Las Vegas Raiders or the Texans have the best shot at making a run at the postseason. The Raiders (4-6) have some winnable games against the Browns, Chargers, Titans and Cardinals, but their trade of QB Kirk Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings may have hurt their chances for success.

The Texans were expected to battle the Jaguars for the division title because they have a potentially dominant defense. They would suddenly have to hit their stride in order to make a run, but that seems unlikely because of the way DeMeco Ryans' team has played to this point.

Cade Cunningham's first-quarter dunk over Victor Wembanyama set the tone for the Pistons to control the rest of the game.

With the game in the final minute, Harper slipped and did a split as he tried to plant his right foot. He remained down on the ground in pain and clutched his groin as the Pistons played 5-on-4 on the other side of the court.

The precise nature and severity of his injury wasn't initially clear, but he needed help getting up before limping to the locker room on his own before the game's conclusion.

Harper's thrived since joining San Antonio's starting lineup in his second NBA season with averages of 18.3 points, 5.5 rebounds and 4.7 assists per game. An extended absence would be a difficult blow for the Spurs on top of Tuesday's loss.

The Spurs are left to regroup from a thorough defeat on their home court and a potentially significant injury that abruptly snapped the high of a 12-game win streak. #2674. When Brian Flores defenses face Kyle Shanahan offenses, Flores is 5-0

Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores will face off with 49ers head coach Kyle Shanahan on Sunday night in Mexico City, and if history is any guide, Flores has the advantage.

Teams with Flores as a head coach or defensive assistant have played against teams with Shanahan as a head coach or offensive assistant five times, and Flores is 5-0.

Since becoming defensive coordinator of the Vikings, Flores has coached against Shanahan’s 49ers twice, with Flores’ team coming out on top both times. In 2023, the Vikings beat the 49ers 22-17. In 2024, the Vikings beat the 49ers 23-17.

Flores and Shanahan coached against each other as head coaches once, in 2020, when Flores’ Dolphins beat Shanahan’s 49ers 43-17.

The most famous game with Flores and Shanahan on opposite sides came in Super Bowl LI, when Flores was linebackers coach for the Patriots and Shanahan was offensive coordinator for the Falcons. Shanahan’s Falcons jumped out to a 28-3 lead, only to have Flores’ Patriots come back and win 34-28 in overtime.

And in 2011, New England beat Washington 34-27 when Flores was a defensive assistant in New England and Shanahan was offensive coordinator in Washington.

Shanahan was once on a coaching staff that got a win against a team with Flores on the staff, in the final game of the 2009 season, when the Patriots had already clinched their playoff spot, rested starters and lost to a Texans team with Shanahan as offensive coordinator. But Flores was a special teams assistant, not a defensive assistant, on that Patriots team, so that game wasn’t a Flores defense vs. Shanahan offense competition.

When it’s Flores coaching defense against Shanahan coaching offense, the result has been the same across different teams and in different coaching roles: Flores has five wins and Shanahan has none.

#2672. Nolan Traore emerging as unlikely star among Nets’ first-round picks

Among seven first-round picks over the past two years, including two in the lottery, few expected Nolan Traore to emerge as the most productive offensively for the Nets this season.

The 19th overall selection in 2025 continues to earn playing time — and the trust of Brooklyn coach Jordi Fernández — with 16.6 points per game over seven November appearances, including 23 (which tied a career high) with four assists and two steals in Monday's home loss to the 76ers.

"I think [it's] the pace that he's playing with, but I also like his voice. His voice is growing," Fernández said about Traore after the game. "He's creating to score, but also he is creating for his teammates. And you see it.

Nolan Traore drives to the basket during the Nets' loss to the 76ers on Nov. 16, 2026, at Barclays Center. NBAE via Getty Images

"I think he had four assists to two turnovers, which is a pretty good ratio. And everything else. He shot the 3 well. He scored at the rim. He's played very well, and I think you can see the growth, and that's contagious. It's his speed and the way he plays … He can make winning plays."

Of course, the Nets haven't won often — they are 3-10 entering Wednesday's home game against the Bulls — but the 20-year-old Traore has earned a spot in Fernández's starting lineup in eight of his 12 appearances this season.

The second-year point guard out of France also is connecting on 42.5 percent of his attempts from 3-point range, up significantly from his .318 percentage from deep in 56 appearances as a rookie.

"I'm just trying to take the shots that the game gives me. I guess it goes well for now and I hope it's gonna keep going," Traore said, before being asked about Fernández's assertion that he is "using his voice" more this season.

"Yeah, I think so," Traore added. "I'm comfortable in the team and in the league, in general, so hopefully it's easier for me to give advice or just tell the guys what we can do better. So yeah, I've noticed it."

Meanwhile, it's impossible not to notice that 6-foot-9 combo guard Egor Dëmin — the eighth overall pick in 2025 — has struggled to find any consistency in his game.

Nolan Traore looks to pass during the Nets' loss to the Knicks on Nov. 2, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. AP Photo/Frank Franklin II

Though this year's lottery pick Mikel Brown Jr. (sixth overall) has shown flashes with 11.0 points in 22 minutes per game off the bench, he has been subjected to a strict minutes restriction in his debut campaign.

The 6-3 Traore similarly has dealt with playing-time limitations since joining the organization.

He also spent 13 games last season playing for the Long Island Nets of the G League.

Nolan Traore dribbles upcourt during the Nets' blowout loss to the Mavericks on Nov. 8, 2026, in Dallas. AP Photo/LM Otero

"He just plays to his strengths," said Julius Randle, who scored 33 points in Monday's loss. "He uses his speed to get downhill and does a good job of getting into the paint, at will.

"From there, he either finishes or finds guys. So he's just doing a good job of playing to his strengths and using his speed."

Traore, who sat out a game last week in New Orleans with a calf injury, matched his season high by logging 30 minutes against the Sixers. But he also sat on the bench for a 10-minute stretch bridging the third and fourth quarters.

"As you guys know, these guys have some sort of restrictions, and obviously I'm not going to tell you what the minutes are," Fernández said. "But we're doing what's best for their bodies, and sometimes it's just like you gotta manage the game.

"There's no excuses here, but I think that Mikel made a couple of shots, [we] let him run, and then I made sure that Nolan was able to finish, and I think that we competed."

Frankly, I think they should make a change. If it gets a lot worse, then you can always change it back. I have nothing against Powers, but he has struggled and it is problematic for sustaining offensive drives. Making a change shouldn't be so hard to do. And maybe there are other things I don't see or cannot understand due to being on the outside looking in. Follow along with the latest live scorecard here as the match unfolds, or click here for key text commentary updates from the action.

Welcome to CricketNews.com's live coverage of the third ODI between Australia and England at the Bellerive Oval in Hobart.

After 46.4 overs, Australia are 231 all out

That's it. Australia are all out for 231 runs as England win by 97 runs and clinch the three-match ODI series 2-1.

It's all but over for Australia as they stare at series defeat with only one wicket in hand and still need 105 runs in the last four overs.

Chasing 329, Australia suffered a disastrous start in the first 10 overs, stumbling to 47/3 (CRR: 4.70) to trail England's 79/1 benchmark at the same stage and leave themselves needing 282 runs from 40 overs (RRR: 7.05).

England's new-ball pairing of Gus Atkinson and Sonny Baker tore through the Australian top order inside the opening two overs. Atkinson dismissed Travis Head in the first over (0.4 ov, 5/1), caught by Joe Root for 4 off 3 balls (1 four), before Baker removed captain Mitchell Marsh in the next over (1.4 ov, 5/2), caught by Harry Brook for 1 off 5 balls.

Baker struck again in the 8th over (7.3 ov, 34/3), having Josh Inglis caught behind by Jordan Cox for 14 off 18 balls (1 four), leaving Matt Renshaw (25* off 30 balls, 4 fours) and Alex Carey (2* off 4 balls) to rebuild.

England added 70 runs across the final 8.3 overs before being bowled out with nine balls remaining, setting Australia a target of 329.

Will Jacks anchored the death-overs push, bringing up a brisk half-century and finishing with 59 off 48 balls (4 fours, 1 six). He forged a 41-run seventh-wicket stand with Sam Curran (19 off 16 balls, 2 fours) before Jack Edwards returned to trigger a late collapse.

Edwards dismissed Curran (44.4 ov, 292/7), Liam Dawson (3 off 3 balls, 46.5 ov, 312/9), and finally Jacks (48.3 ov, 328/10) to finish with superb figures of 4/31 in 5.3 overs.

England accelerated through overs 31 to 40, piling on 82 runs for the loss of two wickets to lift the current run rate back to 6.45 heading into the final 10 overs.

Ben Duckett was the centrepiece of the surge, bringing up a brilliant century before accelerating past three figures with 15 fours and 3 sixes. After Travis Head had Jofra Archer caught by Jack Edwards for 7 off 7 balls (1 four) in the 33rd over (32.1 ov, 195/5), Duckett and Will Jacks forged a brisk 56-run sixth-wicket stand.

Matt Renshaw eventually ended Duckett's marathon knock at 133 off 115 balls in the 39th over.

Australia applied a tight squeeze through overs 21 to 30, restricting England to just 39 runs while claiming two key wickets to bring the run rate down to 5.86.

Cooper Connolly struck early in the phase (22.2 ov), having captain Harry Brook caught by Mahli Beardman for 17 off 23 balls (2 fours) to break a 34-run stand at 146/3. Matt Renshaw then bagged his second wicket in the 29th over (28.6 ov), having Jordan Cox stumped by Josh Inglis for 12 off 23 balls at 172/4.

Amid the slowdown, Ben Duckett held firm at the other end, moving within sight of a century at 84* (82 balls, 12 fours).

England added 58 runs across overs 11 to 20 while losing just one wicket, stabilizing the innings at 6.85 runs per over after Australia pulled back the scoring rate through spin.

Matt Renshaw delivered the breakthrough in the 15th over (14.4), bowling Joe Root for 12 off 15 balls (1 four) to end a 38-run second-wicket partnership at 112/2.

Ben Duckett anchored the middle overs effortlessly, bringing up a composed half-century to reach 62* off 55 balls (10 fours) alongside captain Harry Brook (14* off 15 balls, 2 fours), who helped take 11 runs off Spencer Johnson in the 20th over.

England raced out of the blocks in the first 10 overs against Australia, scoring at 7.90 runs per over on the back of 15 boundaries.

Openers Tom Banton and Ben Duckett put on a brisk 74-run stand before Jack Edwards broke through in the 9th over (8.4), having Banton caught by Mitchell Marsh for 44 off 35 balls (9 fours).

Duckett anchored the rest of the Powerplay unbeaten on 30 (22 balls, 6 fours) alongside Joe Root (0 off 3 balls), while Mahli Beardman closed out the 10th over conceding just 5 runs (2-0-15-0).

Toss result: England won the toss and opted to bat first

Australia and England head into the third and deciding ODI in Hobart with the series level at 1-1 after two one-sided contests. It is the first time since 1979 that the teams have entered the final match of an ODI series in Australia with the score tied.

Bellerive Oval will also host a men's ODI for the first time since 2018 in a game that could offer both sides a valuable confidence boost ahead of next year's World Cup.

Despite missing several frontline players, Australia will look to make home advantage count, but England have an opportunity to secure a rare ODI series victory on Australian soil.

#2666. Gardner Minshew had surgery to repair torn ligament in throwing elbow

Cardinals quarterback Gardner Minshew has had surgery to repair the elbow injury that ended his season.

Minshew had the surgery on his right ulnar collateral ligament on Monday and is expected to make a full recovery, according to ESPN.

Although tearing the UCL is a famously brutal injury for baseball pitchers that necessitated the development of the Tommy John surgery procedure, it tends not to be as severe for the throwing motion of a quarterback. Brock Purdy tore his UCL in the 49ers’ playoff loss in January of 2023, had surgery to repair it in March, and was healthy enough to play the full season and lead the 49ers to the Super Bowl the next year.

Minshew, who will be a free agent in March, should be healthy enough to sign with some team and play in 2027. Where he’ll be playing remains a question.

However, later in the evening, the Chiefs' website also listed Johnson as a third practice squad signing.

Johnson is an eight-year veteran who started his career with the Cincinnati Bengals as an undrafted free agent out of Florida in 2019. He started eight games over three years, but the most time he has seen on the field has been the last four years with the Eagles.

Johnson started in 14 regular-season games in 2024 and 2025; he also played in all four playoff games through beating Kansas City in the Super Bowl. He primarily lined up at right tackle (726 snaps) during that time, but he also played 419 snaps at left tackle.

Earlier in his NFL career, Johnson spent a stretch of time at right guard.

Among the other names the Chiefs tried out, the most notable is Prater, a two-time Pro Bowl kicker who last kicked for the Buffalo Bills at age 41 last year. He made 18 of 20 field goal attempts, missing one of his two attempts from over 50 yards and another from short range. He also missed three point-after attempts.

But Eric Johnson — the former Missouri State Bear — is a four-year NFL veteran with 41 tackles, two tackles for loss and one sack in his career with the Indianapolis Colts and New England Patriots. #2663. Brian Flores: Vikings plan to rotate players in order to deal with altitude in Mexico City

The 49ers are spending the week in Colorado in order to acclimate to the altitude at which their Week 11 game in Mexico City will be played. The Vikings are not.

But the Vikings have a plan for ensuring that their players are ready for the conditions.

“It’s a tough place to play,” Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores told reporters on Tuesday regarding the game in Mexico. “I think it’s certainly taxing, you know, I think you’re — I think as a staff, we’re already talking about, ‘Hey, we’re gonna have to rotate these guys and get, you know, everybody’s gonna have to be ready to play.’ And so, yeah, I mean, it’s certainly something that we’re going to have to deal with.

“But there’s always, you know, elements that we’re dealing with, whether it’s cold or rain or things like that. Obviously, we’re indoors, so we don’t deal with much of that. But that’s the environment we’re gonna be in. I think we prepare them to play in that environment, but I’m not gonna be silly and say, ‘Hey, everybody, hey, you’ve got to go in there for 12 straight plays.’ I mean, we’re not doing that.

“So we’ll have rotations, we’ll have a plan in place. Inevitably, we’re gonna have to fight through some of that. It’s not gonna be your average game where, you know, altitude, you just have — you literally have less oxygen. So we’ve got to do a great job as a staff of making sure — watching guys, and making sure we got guys in and out, you know, as much as we can.”

His broader message to the defense is clear: “We’re gonna need everybody.”

Regardless of the plan to rotate players, the question becomes whether the 49ers will be able to rely more heavily on their best players because they’re properly acclimated to the thinner air. The Vikings are choosing a different strategy, and it’s going to create an obvious apples to apples comparison between the approaches that the two teams have taken — especially if the 49ers players seem to be fresh and the Vikings players seem to be gassed.

#2661. Spurs guard Dylan Harper slips on court late in loss to Pistons, limps off with apparent groin injury

The court at the Frost Bank Center got Dylan Harper on Tuesday night.

With about a minute left in the San Antonio Spurs' 105-93 loss to the Detroit Pistons, Harper suddenly went down on the wing right as Stephon Castle was passing him the ball. While it looked like Harper was maybe hit in real time, a closer look revealed that he just slipped on the court and his legs went opposite ways.

Harper was rolling around on the court holding his groin, clearly in a lot of pain, as play continued the other direction. He eventually stood up and walked back to the locker room after play stopped and the athletic training staff came out, but Harper looked very uncomfortable moving as he worked his way into the tunnel.

"It just looked like he slipped, obviously, so hoping for the best, but don't know anything yet," Spurs head coach Mitch Johnson said, via the San Antonio Express-News' Tom Orsborn.

Harper did not return to the game. He finished with 20 points and four rebounds while shooting 8 of 14 from the field in the loss, which was the Spurs' third of the season and snapped their 12-game win streak.

Harper entered Tuesday's contest averaging 18.3 points, 5.5 rebounds and 4.7 assists per game this season, all of which are improvements from his rookie campaign. The Spurs selected Harper with the No. 2 overall pick in the 2025 NBA Draft out of Rutgers.

The Pistons rallied in the second half Tuesday night to secure the 12-point win in San Antonio. They ended the contest on a 10-3 burst and held the Spurs scoreless in the final 90 seconds.

Cade Cunningham led the way with 27 points in the win, which pushed the Pistons to 9-4 on the season. Daniss Jenkins added 25 points, and Ausar Thompson finished just shy of a triple-double with 14 points, 12 assists, 9 rebounds.

Victor Wembanyama led the Spurs with 25 points and 18 rebounds. He had two ridiculous self-alley-oop dunks in the first quarter. Devin Vassell added 12 points, and Castle finished with 10 points and seven assists.

Though it's unclear how long Harper may be sidelined because of the injury, losing him for any significant stretch will make things much more difficult for the Spurs as they try to remain at the top of the Western Conference standings.

#2657. Jahmyr Gibbs is on pace to break the NFL record for yards from scrimmage in a season

Detroit’s Jahmyr Gibbs hasn’t just been the most productive running back in the NFL this season. He may produce more yardage than any player in NFL history this season.

Through nine games this season, Gibbs has an NFL-high 1,000 rushing yards. Gibbs also has 384 receiving yards. That total of 1,384 yards from scrimmage leads the league, and with an average of 153.8 yards per game, Gibbs is on pace total 2,614 yards from scrimmage over a 17-game season.

That would break the NFL record, held by Chris Johnson of the Titans, who totaled 2,509 yards from scrimmage (2,006 rushing yards and 503 receiving yards) in 2009.

Gibbs has touched the ball an NFL-high 240 times this season, combining 199 carries and 41 catches. With that kind of workload, the biggest concern for the Lions will be whether he can stay healthy for 17 regular-season games and, the Lions hope, some playoff games after that.

But given how well Gibbs is playing, it would be hard for Dan Campbell and the Lions’ coaching staff to reduce his workload. He’s having a special season. He’s halfway to one of the greatest seasons any running back has ever had.

#2655. Pro athletes clamor for 'Grand Theft Auto VI' release, but they insist their day jobs come first

LOS ANGELES (AP) — When "Grand Theft Auto VI" finally drops this week, professional athletes across the globe will immediately begin stealing cars, running from the cops and causing every manner of virtual mayhem under the Vice City sun.

The love affair between pro athletes and video games has been strong for decades now, and the most anticipated game release in over a decade is certain to cause sleep-deprived nights and create mostly friendly competition across locker rooms and clubhouses — even if everybody insists they won't let it be a distraction from their day jobs.

"I told my mom she might not hear from me for a couple of days," Los Angeles Rams receiver Tutu Atwell said with a grin.

Atwell is an avid gamer and a Miami native who has been playing the GTA series since childhood, so the release of this iteration set in a fictional Florida is monumental. He preordered "GTA VI" to appear on his console right at midnight — and he even looked into changing his region setting to New Zealand so he could get the game 21 hours earlier, but ultimately decided it wasn't worth the hassle.

"We've been waiting for this one for a long, long time," Atwell said. "You know it's going to be a big one."

Pro athletes become avid gamers for obvious reasons: They're nearly all competition junkies with plenty of money and thousands of hours of free time to game after practices, in road hotels and throughout their offseasons.

Athletes from Kyler Murray to Karl-Anthony Towns have amassed large followings on the livestreaming platform Twitch, playing games like the NBA 2K and Madden titles or first-person shooters like "Fortnite" and "Call of Duty" before tens of thousands of viewers. Formula 1 champions Max Verstappen and Lando Norris often seem just as enthusiastic about simulated racing as the real thing. Some athletes even become elite gamers: Lakers superstar Luka Doncic, for instance, was a top-500 player in the tank role on "Overwatch 2" two years ago.

But no series dazzles a wider spectrum of the gaming public than GTA — and its long absence from the market only made these athletes hungrier.

Rockstar Games released "GTA V" in 2013, when a large chunk of the world's current pro athletes were adolescents, and the release of "GTA VI" has been tantalizingly delayed for several years.

Several weeks ago, Houston Rockets center Steven Adams predicted a seismic disruption to all sports when the game finally arrived.

"You guys should anticipate November, the performance of the league, league-wide ... GTA is dropping," Adams said. "So look at NFL, look at all the leagues and watch them plummet, mate. Just for the month of November, performance is going to go down. It should come back up, though. Most of the boys will get it done, hopefully they finish the game. Coaching as well, by the way. The coaching and scouts are going to be horrendous, mate. Just for November, but it should bounce back in December, hopefully. Should be good."

Whether he was joking or not, Adams nonetheless set off rampant chatter about what the game's release will mean for athlete and workplace productivity.

When asked directly, most high-level athletes say they're too serious about sports to develop a severe case of GTA Flu. That's the term coined in 2013 when untold thousands of gamers took days off work to dive headfirst into San Andreas, the game's fictionalized version of Southern California.

Los Angeles Rams left guard Steve Avila doesn't expect athletes to shirk their primary duties, but he knows the game will still affect plenty of pros in more subtle ways.

"I think a lot of athletes are responsible," Avila said. "But if you could check guys' sleep scores ... I think you'd see that some guys' hours are going to go way down, for sure."

Avila is a big admirer of Rockstar's artistic ambitions along with GTA's famously frenetic game play. He had never been to California before he played "Grand Theft Auto," so he and his friends have been repeatedly surprised in recent years to see the real versions of Los Angeles-area landmarks they already recognized from speeding past them in a stolen car while being chased by virtual cops.

The Rams players are among the fortunate few athletes who are on their bye week when the game is released — and Avila is even on injured reserve, although he laughingly insists his rehab work will supersede GTA.

Jets running back Breece Hall echoes both Avila's enthusiasm for the game and his belief the day job comes first. Hall's mother only allowed him to play sports video games, so he has been waiting a decade for the chance to immerse himself in a chapter of the GTA franchise.

"Yeah, definitely getting the film done early, and then spending the extra hour or two on the game," Hall said. "But you've got to make sure you handle the main thing first before you have some fun."

Phoenix Suns guard Devin Booker is an inveterate gamer who travels with a gaming laptop and has played "Grand Theft Auto" since before multiplayer options were available. The five-time NBA All-Star loves to "go in there and just be reckless," he said with a laugh.

Like Atwell, Booker has already preordered "GTA VI."

The game is a childhood throwback for younger pros like Anaheim Ducks star Leo Carlsson, who played earlier versions for untold hours as a kid in Sweden. He'll dive into it when the Ducks return from a road trip — and like many athletes, he's eagerly anticipating the game's multiplayer component, which isn't expected to be available until 2027.

"I love playing with teammates," Carlsson said. "If they dropped (GTA) in the summer, it would probably take over huge time in our life."

"GTA VI" is set in a chimerical Florida called Leonida, which contains Vice City – the franchise's stand-in for Miami.

And the NBA's Miami Heat are at the epicenter of the release when they hold "A Night in Vice City" on Wednesday.

The Heat have topped Kaseya Center with a "Vice City" neon sign as an advertisement for the game, and they'll wear themed alternate uniforms when they face Milwaukee on a special court. The entire night in downtown Miami will have a GTA theme.

"It's a big thing because growing up as a kid, we all were on 'Grand Theft Auto,'" Heat forward Bobby Portis said. "It's been like a buildup, and everybody's been asking when this was going to come out. Just so happy that we're here in Miami when it does. Definitely an honor to be able to grace the Vice City and be a part of history."

Heat forward Keshad Johnson said he played the GTA franchise "all my life growing up. It's been so long since the last one came out. But for there to be a new one and to be a part of it, that's gonna be fire. That's gonna be dope. … I've seen the jerseys. Elite."

AP Basketball Writer Tim Reynolds in Miami and AP Sports Writers Dennis Waszak Jr. in New York, Pat Graham in Denver and David Brandt in Phoenix contributed to this report.

#2654. Dan Campbell: I'm not telling players to stop celebrating because of Aidan Hutchinson's injury

Lions head coach Dan Campbell isn’t going to tell his players to stop celebrating in the wake of defensive end Aidan Hutchinson’s torn ACL.

Hutchinson will miss the rest of the season after injuring his right knee while celebrating a sack of Drake Maye in Sunday’s game. Hutchinson jumped in the air and immediately grabbed his right knee after suffering the injury when he landed back on the turf in Munich.

During an appearance on 97.1 The Ticket on Tuesday, Campbell called the injury “one of those things that happened” and that “this game is built off of emotion” that he doesn’t want to take away from his players.

“I hate it, I know he hates it. But I mean, the number of moves that I’ve seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one leg lateral movements, lateral balances, I’m just like, it was one of those things, man,” Campbell said. “I hate it. I think we all understand what it is, and let’s be smart, but no, I’m not gonna say, ‘Nobody celebrates anymore.’”

Campbell recounted a story from his own playing days when he tore his biceps while celebrating a play and that didn’t lead him to make celebration avoidance a rule for the Lions when he got the job. That doesn’t make Hutchinson’s injury any easier for the Lions to deal with, but it makes his response to questions about the advice he’ll give in the future easier to understand.

#2647. Seahawks owner Vinod Khosla: "We’re very happy with both the team and the location right now"

A pattern has emerged during the early months of Vinod Khosla’s ownership of the Seahawks.

First, he’ll make a less-than-unequivocal comment about the future of the team in Seattle. Two, people will hear his words and repeat them. Three, he will complain about that.

Most recently, Khosla was asked directly during an appearance on CNBC whether he’d consider moving the team.

“I’ve not made any plans or even discussed -- I know the press like to make a big deal of it,” Khosla said, via Sports Business Journal. “We’re very happy with both the team and the location right now.”

And there it is. “Right now.” Why add “right now”? Just end the sentence at “location.”

The same thing happened last month, when Khosla was interviewed by Axios. Asked point-blank whether he’d make a commitment to keeping the Seahawks in Seattle over the long term, Khosla said, “I will never say never. Will this team be in Seattle 500 years from now? But I have no current plans to change anything about the team, including the coach, president, operation or Lumen Field. It all functions well.”

It’s not difficult (although apparently it is). The question is simple. Will you move from Seattle?

While there’s an element of taxpayer-funding leverage that arises regarding future stadium renovations and/or construction in Seattle, there’s a way to answer the question that doesn’t sacrifice future bargaining power.

“We have every intention to remain in Seattle” is the easiest reply. With no further elaboration or explanation.

Frankly, his latest comments are more ominous than his response to the Axios question. Beyond the fact that he now knows anything he says will be repeated (indeed, he prefaced his response by saying “the press like to make a big deal of it”), Khosla added the ultimate caveat that will cause local consternation.

Right now isn’t “500 years from now.” Right now is subject to change. Because right now applies only to — wait for it — right now.

Right now is later from the last time I typed “right now.”

Here’s what Khosla should do: Write a check right now to someone who will give him a script to recite whenever he’s asked that question in the future right now.

SAN ANTONIO — Cade Cunningham scored 27 points, Daniss Jenkins had 21 of his 25 in the second half and the Detroit Pistons snapped San Antonio's 12-game winning streak, toppling the Spurs 105-93 on Tuesday night.

Victor Wembanyama had 25 points and 18 rebounds for San Antonio, which had not lost since Oct. 23, when it fell to 0-2.

Spurs guard Dylan Harper finished with 20 points but exited with 53 seconds remaining after his left foot slid out from under him and he tumbled to the court. His status is unknown.

Ausur Thompson finished with 14 points, 12 assists, nine rebounds and five steals for Detroit, which overcame a sluggish start to win after getting swept by San Antonio last season.

The Pistons outscored the Spurs 52-39 in the second and third quarters while shooting 51%. San Antonio lost for the first time when leading by double digits.

Wembanyama had 10 points in the opening quarter, highlighted by throwing the ball off the backboard over an outstretched Thompson to assist on his own left-handed dunk.

San Antonio had five dunks in the first quarter but it was Cunningham who provided the exclamation with a one-handed slam against a late-closing Wembanyama.

Detroit went on a 21-7 run in the second quarter, closing with a 13-5 burst when Wembanyama sat, to tie the game at 47 after trailing by 14 in the period. The Spurs outscored the Pistons by 12 when Wembanyama was on the court in the first half and were minus-12 when he sat.

Thompson and Cunningham combined for 21 points in the second quarter as Detroit outscored San Antonio 29-21 in taking its first lead at 55-54 into halftime.

Detroit was without Jalen Duren (strained right hamstring), Duncan Robinson (strained left quadriceps) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

#2637. Pressing Impossible: Amorim Loses to Hürzeler – MH

von Next Generation am 21.01.2025 in den Kategorien Premier League mit 0 Kommentaren

Rúben Amorim’s Manchester United faced Hürzeler’s Brighton on Matchday 22. While Manchester United focused on creating a pressing system that was as difficult as possible to bypass, Brighton once again showcased their incredibly challenging vertical play. You can find the tactical analysis of the match here.

Manchester United started in a nominal 3-2-4-1 formation, which transitioned into a classic 3-2-5 shape in possession and a 5-4-1 shape out of possession. Amorim initially placed particular emphasis on a pressing system that varied in height.

Meanwhile, Brighton began in a nominal 4-4-2 formation. In possession, Hürzeler’s trademark positional rotations, vertical runs, and decoy movements for effective vertical play were continuously on display. Accordingly, Brighton operated flexibly from a variety of shapes, such as 4-2-4, 3-1-4-2, 2-3-2-3, and more. This flexibility makes Brighton’s possession game almost impossible to define through formations alone.

Manchester United initially operated out of a 5-4-1 shape with varying defensive heights. This approach made it challenging for the opposition to play through, as different build-up phases required distinct patterns and connections. United’s central midfielders in the four-man line marked Brighton’s double pivot, Ayari and Baleba, man-to-man. The goal was to gain optimal control of Brighton’s central build-up play and apply immediate pressure whenever Brighton played through the middle.

However, Brighton controlled the game during the first 10 minutes. Their holding midfielders positioned themselves close to the backline, creating a large gap between Manchester United’s five-man defensive line and the four-man midfield. This space was then exploited with various runs. When facing high pressing, left-back Estupiñán moved asymmetrically into the left half-space behind United’s first pressing block. This movement utilized the moment when Amad stepped out to press one of Brighton’s center-backs after bypassing Zirkzee.

United failed to adjust their pressing to this build-up variation throughout the match. As a result, this same pattern played a key role in Brighton’s build-up to the goal that made it 1-3.

Against Manchester United’s mid-block, Brighton’s strikers, Welbeck and João Pedro, alternated their runs into the large space between the lines, targeting the gaps. This repeatedly allowed Brighton to bypass United’s first pressing line.

The 1-0 goal resulted from this setup, facilitated by a counter-movement. As striker Welbeck made a run into the space between the lines, he pulled his marker, De Ligt, a few steps out of the five-man backline. Simultaneously, winger Mitoma made a deep run into the space that opened up. De Ligt was caught in a dilemma between applying pressure and covering the depth. A long ball bypassed United’s entire pressing structure, leading to the goal.

Another reason for Brighton’s dominance in the first 10 minutes lay in their defensive work. United built up in a very positionally structured 3-2-4-1(/-5) shape. Brighton countered with a 4-4-2 in their high press and a 5-4-1 in their mid-block. With Minteh marking Diogo Dalot man-to-man on the flank, the two different defensive shapes emerged depending on Dalot’s positioning. Brighton’s two strikers also pressed United’s center-backs, Maguire and Yoro, on that side, applying intense pressure on the ball carriers on United’s left.

On the other side, winger Mitoma positioned himself centrally when De Ligt had possession, cutting off the passing lane to Amad, who was positioned in the half-space. If the ball was played to full-back Mazraoui, Mitoma shifted out from the center to press, creating a cover shadow into the center. This approach effectively prevented United from creating any significant attacking threat on that flank as well.

Around the 10th minute, the game shifted, and Brighton’s dominance began to fade. This was due to several adjustments made by United. To reduce the pressure on the ball carrier on the left side, the highly press-resistant Bruno Fernandes started to drop deeper, while the full-backs pushed higher. The resulting 3-3-4 shape was difficult for Brighton to press, as full-back Veltman, or the half-space defender in the five-man backline, was pulled out, and the space in behind could be exploited by either full-back Dalot or striker Zirkzee. This led to more possession phases for United and fewer pressing phases from Brighton.

Another adjustment United made came from their 5-4-1 mid-block. The half-space defenders, De Ligt and Yoro, became much more aggressive in stepping out from the five-man backline to defend against the strikers making runs into the large space between the lines. This prevented Brighton’s strikers from turning after bypassing the first pressing block. To defend long balls to Mitoma, Mazraoui prioritized covering the space behind the advancing De Ligt. As a result, Brighton struggled to exploit the space between the lines and couldn’t progress as effectively into depth from their own possession.

Additionally, Manchester United no longer pressed situationally from a 5-4-1 in a high press but instead switched to man-marking, with Zirkzee following the goalkeeper. This created much more pressure on the ball carriers for Brighton. Brighton also faced difficulties because the 5-4-1 system is much harder to break down than man-marking in a high press. This became evident in the sequence leading to the penalty goal. United suddenly switched to man-marking and forced the ball loss that preceded the penalty. United deservedly equalized.

After the 1-1, the game became more balanced. Brighton tried to break down the 5-4-1 mid-block, while United focused on their defensive game. When Manchester United switched to man-marking in the high press, Brighton continued to struggle and often lost possession. It is questionable why United didn’t press more boldly and high man-to-man during this phase to maintain better control of the game.

From Brighton’s perspective, a situational, more extreme overload at the moment of transition into high pressing—such as in the central space just behind the first pressing block—could have been a possible solution to challenge United’s man-marking, putting them in a dilemma between applying pressure and defending 1v1 on the wings.

In possession, Brighton’s players frequently adjusted their positioning with small movements, creating new passing lanes, changing passing angles, and occupying spaces that forced the opponent into pressing reactions. Below, I have outlined the main advantages of three of the most frequently used shapes by Brighton against United, as well as the necessary reactions from the opponent.

Another factor that makes Brighton’s vertical play difficult to defend is the continuous variety of space-creating or counter-movement runs into depth. These consistently create dilemmas and positional problems for the defenders. Below, I have graphically illustrated some selected runs used against United:

Despite Brighton’s possession game, Manchester United was able to prevent significant goal-scoring opportunities initially. From their 5-4-1 structure, the center-backs repeatedly defended aggressively, stepping out. Additionally, after bypassing the four-man midfield, it would drop back into position. This “recovery defense” made it difficult for Brighton to develop effective build-up play and exploit opened spaces. Furthermore, United’s players were well-prepared for potential through balls. In particular, the full-backs consistently intercepted balls played behind the defensive line.

Towards the end of the first half and into the second half, small (forced?) mistakes began to creep into United’s pressing. While the failure to step out with Ugarte in the man-marking high press in the 39th minute and the failure to step out with De Ligt in the space between the lines in the 38th minute went unpunished, the delayed step-out by Yoro led to Brighton’s deserved 1-2 at that point.

Especially in the second half, Brighton adapted to the man-marking high press by having center-backs step inside, breaking free from the cover shadow, when pressing the goalkeeper. This allowed them to find the open player in build-up, and Brighton lost possession less frequently.

Longer defensive phases in the mid-block for Manchester United followed. To break down this block, Brighton’s possession play became less positionally rigid. Much more frequently, certain zones were overloaded. In particular, the space between the lines and the half-spaces were repeatedly occupied by multiple players to create numerical superiority and apply pressure.

United, on the other hand, struggled to create any significant goal-scoring chances. The high press could have been more effective with a brief orientation from the player pressing the goalkeeper to position the inward-dropping center-back into a cover shadow. From the 5-4-1 mid-block, too few dangerous ball recoveries and transition situations were created.

Even from their own possession, Manchester United created too few goal-scoring opportunities. When Amorim’s team did get chances, it was typically through a ball played behind the wide players of the five-man backline. At the moment the press was triggered (when Brighton stepped out to the outer center-back), the forward half-space player dropped deep, and the winger made a run in behind. However, the lack of other ways to break the press led to too much predictability in United’s possession play.

As a result, the second half became more one-sided, with Brighton repeatedly pressing and trying to put United’s press under pressure. This ultimately succeeded, leading to a deserved 3-1 victory for Hürzeler’s team.

Amorim’s press, which was difficult to break down, was ultimately cracked by Hürzeler. It is questionable whether more frequent defensive pressing, alternating with situational man-marking high pressing, might have been a better approach against Brighton, in order to better tighten the space between the lines and the area behind the defense, making the game in depth more difficult. Despite a higher mid-block, Manchester United hardly created any dangerous transition situations. This is an area that needs improvement.

Hürzeler’s team, on the other hand, once again played highly attractive possession football. Frequent positional rotations, shifts in formations, overloads, and space-creating runs make Brighton’s possession game very enjoyable to watch. For the opponent, these constant shifts in zones over an extended period are incredibly difficult to defend. It is very likely that we will see more teams adopting similar principles in possession in the future.

Author: “MH” is a young analyst who regularly produces content for Spielverlagerung NextGen and TotalFootballAnalysis. On X, he can be found under Mh_sv5.

#2635. Fred Johnson finds new NFL home after being cut by the Eagles

Former Philadelphia Eagles swing tackle and Super Bowl LIX champion Fred Johnson is signing with the Kansas City Chiefs' practice squad, as noted by our friends over at Arrowhead Pride.

The Eagles notably waived Johnson and Hollywood Brown late last week. Neither player was claimed off waivers, making them free agents available to sign with any team.

KC is a nice landing spot for Johnson. The 29-year-old blocker is going from a No. 1 overall pick in the 2027 NFL Draft contender to a Super Bowl contender. And he might actually find some playing time since the Chiefs are dealing with some offensive line woes. Maybe Big Fred can get another championship ring by winning with Big Red instead of beating him this time.

As for the Eagles, they're seemingly moved forward with John Ojukwu as their new swing tackle. The team signed him from their practice squad to their roster on Monday. If he has to fill in for either Jordan Mailata or Markel Bell, maybe he'll show some potential to be the Eagles' next Fred Johnson.

Coach JJ Redick said the Lakers star guard will get on the floor on Wednesday ahead of the game against the Jazz before his availability is determined.

Austin Reaves of the Los Angeles Lakers arrives at Fiserv Forum in Milwaukee, Wisconsin, on Wednesday, Nov. 11, before a game against the Bucks. NBAE via Getty Images

Reaves was sidelined for the second straight game because of a left heel bruise/contusion he suffered during the third quarter of the Lakers' Nov. 11 road win over the Bucks.

Redick was consistent with his previous comments that Reaves' availability will be determined by his pain tolerance.

Austin Reaves of the Los Angeles Lakers looks on during a game against the Detroit Pistons in Detroit, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Kevon Looney got his first start of the season Tuesday. NBAE via Getty Images
